\displaystyle\quad+C_{1}\left[\left(1-\frac{2p}{n}\right)\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{p}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{n-p}{n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2}\left[\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+n\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right].

We now group the terms by the error components
\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}
,
\sum\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}
, and the stepsize
\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}+C_{1}\left(1-\frac{2p}{n}\right)\right)\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(C_{1}\frac{p}{n^{3}}+C_{2}\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+C_{1}\frac{n-p}{n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}+C_{2}n\left(\frac{n-p}{n}\right)\delta_{2}^{2}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Substituting
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
back into the coefficients reveals the simplifications:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{\gamma n}{4p}-\frac{\gamma n}{4p}\cdot\frac{2p}{n}\right)\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\gamma n}{4p}\cdot\frac{p}{n^{3}}+\frac{\gamma}{4np}\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{\gamma n}{4p}\cdot\frac{n-p}{n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{4np}\cdot(n-p)\delta_{2}^{2}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=f(x^{t})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{\gamma n}{4p}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)}_{=\frac{\gamma n}{4p}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left(\frac{\gamma}{4n^{2}}+\frac{\gamma}{4np}-\frac{\gamma}{4n^{2}}\right)}_{=\frac{\gamma}{4np}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{\gamma(n-p)}{4np}\delta_{2}^{2}+\frac{\gamma(n-p)}{4np}\delta_{2}^{2}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Recognizing that the coefficients for the gradient error terms match the definition of
\Psi^{t}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{t}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{\gamma(n-p)}{2np}\delta_{2}^{2}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Hence, the last term is nonpositive whenever

\displaystyle L-\frac{1}{\gamma}+\gamma\,\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n-p}{np}\leq 0\mathchar 59\relax

that is, whenever

\displaystyle\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n-p}{np}\gamma^{2}+L\gamma\leq 1.

By Lemma
6
, applied with

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle:=\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n-p}{np}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad b:=L\mathchar 59\relax

it therefore suffices to impose

\displaystyle\gamma\leq\frac{1}{L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n-p}{np}}}\mathchar 59\relax

which is exactly the stepsize condition in the theorem.

Assuming that this condition holds, and unrolling the recursion, we obtain, for every
T\geq 0
, by defining
\tilde{x}^{T}
as
x^{t}
for
t
chosen uniformly at random in
\{0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}T\}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(\tilde{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\Psi^{0}}{\gamma(T+1)}.

∎

The following corollary turns Theorem
1
into a component-gradient complexity bound when the initial estimators are arbitrary.
It then specializes the bound to the choice
p=n/m
, which gives the stated complexity in the regime
m\geq n
.

See
1

Proof of Corollary
1
.

Theorem
1
gives

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(\tilde{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{2\Psi^{0}}{\gamma(T+1)}.

Hence, if
T+1\geq 2\Psi^{0}/(\gamma\epsilon)
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(\tilde{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\epsilon.

Since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma}

\displaystyle=L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n-p}{np}}\leq L+\frac{\delta_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\mathchar 59\relax

the required number of iterations satisfies

\displaystyle T={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\frac{\delta_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\frac{\Psi^{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

It remains to count component-gradient evaluations. At one iteration of
Algorithm
1
, there are two cases. If
\theta^{t}=0
, which happens
with probability
1-p
, the algorithm updates every group estimator using one
sample
j_{i}^{t}\in[m]
per group
i\in[n]
. For each such group it computes

\displaystyle\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

so this case costs
2n
component-gradient evaluations.

If
\theta^{t}=1
, which happens with probability
p
, the algorithm samples one
group
i^{t}\in[n]
and computes the full group gradient

\displaystyle\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})\mathchar 59\relax

which costs
m
component-gradient evaluations. For the remaining
n-1
groups,
the algorithm performs the same two-point component-gradient update as above,
costing
2(n-1)
additional component-gradient evaluations. Therefore, this
case costs
m+2(n-1)
component-gradient evaluations.

Thus the expected number of component-gradient evaluations per iteration is

\displaystyle(1-p)\,2n+p\left(m+2(n-1)\right)

\displaystyle=2n+pm-2p={\cal O}\left(n+pm\right).

Multiplying this expected per-iteration cost by the iteration bound yields

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left((n+pm)\left(L+\frac{\delta_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\right)\frac{\Psi^{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

Finally, because
m\geq n
, the choice
p=n/m
belongs to
(0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}1]
. Substituting

p=n/m
gives

\displaystyle n+pm

\displaystyle=n+\frac{n}{m}m=2n\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle L+\frac{\delta_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}

\displaystyle=L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}.

Hence

\displaystyle(n+pm)\left(L+\frac{\delta_{2}}{\sqrt{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=2n\left(L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=2nL+2\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\mathchar 59\relax

which gives

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left(\left(nL+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Psi_{n/m}^{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

We now look at the case of exact initialization.

Since
m\geq n
, the choice
p=n/m
belongs to
(0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}1]
. For this value of
p
, the stepsize condition in Theorem
1
gives

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\left(L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n-p}{np}}\right)^{-1}=\left(L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n-\frac{n}{m}}{n\frac{n}{m}}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m-1}{n}}\right)^{-1}.

Next, the exact initialization
g_{i}^{0}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})
for every
i\in[n]
implies

\displaystyle g^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{0}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})=\nabla f(x^{0}).

Therefore, both initialization-error terms in the Lyapunov quantity from Theorem
1
vanish:

\displaystyle\left\|g^{0}-\nabla f(x^{0})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=0\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{0}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

Hence, for
p=n/m
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{n/m}^{0}

\displaystyle=f(x^{0})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}=\Delta_{0}.

Thus, setting
p=n/m
and using
\Psi_{n/m}^{0}=\Delta_{0}
gives the post-initialization iteration complexity

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\frac{\delta_{2}}{\sqrt{n/m}}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{m}{n}}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

The same corollary gives the expected post-initialization component-gradient complexity

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left(\left(nL+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

It remains to add the initialization cost. Computing
g_{i}^{0}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})
requires evaluating the full group gradient

\displaystyle\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{0})\mathchar 59\relax

which costs
m
component-gradient evaluations for a fixed group
i
. Repeating this for all
i\in[n]
costs

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}m

\displaystyle=mn

component-gradient evaluations. Therefore, the total expected component-gradient complexity, including initialization, is

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left(nm+\left(nL+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

∎

C.2
Analysis under the Polyak–Łojasiewicz condition

We next record the PŁ specialization for the case
m\geq n
.
The algorithm and the estimator recursion are unchanged, but the PŁ inequality turns the descent estimate into a contraction for the objective gap.
Thus the accuracy dependence becomes
O\left(\cdot\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)
for reaching
{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]\leq\epsilon
, instead of the
O\left(\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)
dependence in the preceding general nonconvex stationarity bounds.
The theorem is stated under a global PŁ condition.
However, the proof uses this condition only at the iterates, so the same argument applies on any PŁ region that contains the trajectory.

We state the PŁ condition once here because it is shared by the PŁ analyses in both regimes.

Assumption 5

(Polyak–Łojasiewicz (PŁ) condition)
.

The function
f
satisfies the PŁ-condition with parameter
\mu>0
if

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla f(x)\right\|^{2}\geq 2\mu\big(f(x)-f^{*}\big)\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mathchar 59\relax

(18)

where
f^{*}:=\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x)>-\infty
.

The following lemma is a generic PŁ recursion.
It lets us separate the algorithm-specific Lyapunov calculation from the final step that converts such a recursion into linear convergence.

Lemma 8

(
[
43
]
)
.

Suppose that Assumption
5
holds (implying
f^{*}>-\infty
). Let
\{\Psi^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a sequence of nonnegative real numbers. Suppose that for constants
C\geq 0
,
\mu>0
, and stepsize
\gamma\in(0\mathchar 59\relax 1/\mu)
, the following inequality holds for all
t\geq 0
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\gamma\Psi^{t+1}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+(1-\gamma\mu)\gamma\Psi^{t}+\gamma C.

Then, for all
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]\leq(1-\gamma\mu)^{T}\left((f(x^{0})-f^{*})+\gamma\Psi^{0}\right)+\frac{C}{\mu}.

(19)

Proof.

Subtracting
f^{*}
from both sides of the assumed inequality and applying the PŁ-condition (
18
) (specifically,
-\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\leq-\mu(f(x^{t})-f^{*})
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})-f^{*}\right]+\gamma\Psi^{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})-f^{*}\right]-\gamma\mu{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})-f^{*}\right]+(1-\gamma\mu)\gamma\Psi^{t}+\gamma C

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma\mu)\left({\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})-f^{*}\right]+\gamma\Psi^{t}\right)+\gamma C.

Unrolling this recursion from
t=T-1
down to
0
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]+\gamma\Psi^{T}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\mu)^{T}\left((f(x^{0})-f^{*})+\gamma\Psi^{0}\right)+\gamma C\sum_{k=0}^{T-1}(1-\gamma\mu)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\mu)^{T}\left((f(x^{0})-f^{*})+\gamma\Psi^{0}\right)+\frac{C}{\mu}\mathchar 59\relax

where in the last step we bounded the finite geometric series by the infinite sum
\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(1-\gamma\mu)^{k}=\frac{1}{\gamma\mu}
.
Finally, using the nonnegativity of
\Psi^{T}
and
\gamma
, we have
{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]+\gamma\Psi^{T}
, which yields the desired result.
∎

The next theorem is the PŁ counterpart of Theorem
1
.
It applies Lemma
8
to Algorithm
1
after choosing Lyapunov weights that match the estimator recursions from Lemma
7
.

Theorem 3

(Linear convergence under PŁ, case
m\geq n
)
.

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
,
4
, and
5
hold,
where Assumption
5
holds with parameter
\mu>0
.
Run Algorithm
1
with probability
p\in(0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}1]
, arbitrary initial estimators

g_{1}^{0}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}g_{n}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
g^{0}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{0}
.
Let the stepsize be set as

0<\gamma\leq\min\left\{\left(L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{2(n-p)}{np}}\right)^{-1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\,\frac{p}{2\mu n}\right\}
.
Then, for every
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]\leq(1-\gamma\mu)^{T}\left(\Psi_{\mathrm{PL}}^{0}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

(20)

where

\Psi_{\mathrm{PL}}^{0}:=f(x^{0})-f^{*}+\frac{\gamma n}{3p}\left\|g^{0}-\nabla f(x^{0})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2\gamma}{3p}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{0}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\right\|^{2}.

Proof of Theorem
3
.

Let us fix constants
\nu\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\rho\in[0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\infty)
that we will define later.
We start from the descent lemma for
L
-smooth functions, applied with
x^{t+1}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}
:

\displaystyle f(x^{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

(21)

Taking expectation and adding
\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]
and

\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]
to both sides, and then using Lemma
7
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]+\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(1-\frac{2p}{n}\right)\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{p}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{n-p}{n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right].

Rearranging the terms yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]+\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}+\nu\left(1-\frac{2p}{n}\right)\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\rho\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)+\nu\frac{p}{n^{2}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\nu\frac{n-p}{n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}+\rho\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\delta_{2}^{2}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right].

(22)

We now choose

\displaystyle\nu:=\frac{\gamma n}{3p}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\rho:=\frac{2\gamma}{3p}.

(23)

With this choice, the coefficient of
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right]
in (
22
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\nu\frac{n-p}{n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}+\rho\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)\delta_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{\gamma(n-p)}{pn}\delta_{2}^{2}.

Thus, the term is nonpositive whenever

\displaystyle L-\frac{1}{\gamma}+2\gamma\,\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n-p}{pn}\leq 0\mathchar 59\relax

that is, whenever

\displaystyle 2\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n-p}{pn}\gamma^{2}+L\gamma\leq 1.

By Lemma
6
, applied with

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle:=2\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n-p}{pn}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad b:=L\mathchar 59\relax

it therefore suffices to impose

\displaystyle\gamma\leq\left(L+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{2(n-p)}{np}}\right)^{-1}\mathchar 59\relax

which is exactly the first constraint in the stepsize condition of Theorem
3
.

Hence, we can drop the
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right]
term in (
22
).

Next, with the choice (
23
), we claim that if
\gamma\leq\frac{p}{2\mu n}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}+\nu\left(1-\frac{2p}{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\mu)\nu\mathchar 59\relax

(24)

\displaystyle\rho\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)+\nu\frac{p}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\mu)\rho.

(25)

Indeed, for (
24
),

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}+\nu\left(1-\frac{2p}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{\gamma n}{3p}-\frac{2\gamma}{3}=\frac{\gamma n}{3p}-\frac{\gamma}{6}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma n}{3p}-\gamma^{2}\mu\frac{n}{3p}=(1-\gamma\mu)\nu.

For (
25
), we have

\displaystyle\rho\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)+\nu\frac{p}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma}{3p}\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)+\frac{\gamma n}{3p}\cdot\frac{p}{n^{2}}=\frac{2\gamma}{3p}\left(1-\frac{p}{n}\right)+\frac{\gamma}{3n}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\gamma}{3p}\left(1-\frac{p}{2n}\right)\leq\frac{2\gamma}{3p}(1-\gamma\mu)=(1-\gamma\mu)\rho.

Substituting (
23
), (
24
), and (
25
) into (
22
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\frac{\gamma n}{3p}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{2\gamma}{3p}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(1-\gamma\mu)\left(\frac{\gamma n}{3p}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{2\gamma}{3p}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]\right).

(26)

Finally, define the nonnegative sequence

\displaystyle\Psi^{t}:=\frac{n}{3p}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{2}{3p}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right].

(27)

Then (
26
) reads

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\gamma\Psi^{t+1}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+(1-\gamma\mu)\gamma\Psi^{t}.

In view of Lemma
8
(with
C=0
), we conclude that for every
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]\leq(1-\gamma\mu)^{T}\left((f(x^{0})-f^{*})+\gamma\Psi^{0}\right).

Substituting (
27
) at
t=0
yields (
20
).
∎

Appendix D
SILAGE
, case
n>m

D.1
Implementation-efficient form and equivalence

The algorithm below is an implementation-efficient form of Algorithm
2
.
It avoids explicitly updating all table entries by storing a shared shift, while keeping the same iterates as the analysis-friendly version.

Algorithm 3

SILAGE
(
new
), case
n>m
, implementation-efficient form

1:

input:
stepsize
\gamma
, batch size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\in[n]

2:

init:

x^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
h_{1}^{0}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}h_{n}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
q^{0}:=0

3:

h^{0}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{0}

4:

for

t=0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots

do

5:

x^{t+1}:=x^{t}-\gamma\left(h^{t}+q^{t}\right)

6:

pick
i^{t}\in[n]
uniformly at random

7:

pick
j_{i^{t}}^{t}\in[m]
uniformly at random

8:

compute
\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})
and retain
\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})

9:

pick
\Omega^{t}\subset[n]\setminus\{i^{t}\}
uniformly,
|\Omega^{t}|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1

10:

\tilde{\Omega}^{t}:=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}

11:

for

i\in\Omega^{t}

do

12:

pick
j_{i}^{t}\in[m]
uniformly at random

13:

\Delta_{i}^{t}:=\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})

14:

end

for

15:

\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}:=\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t})

16:

d^{t}:=\frac{1}{b}_{\mathrm{grp}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}

17:

q^{t+1}:=q^{t}+d^{t}

18:

h^{t+1}:=h^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-h_{i^{t}}^{t}-q^{t}-\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)

19:

h_{i^{t}}^{t+1}:=\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-q^{t+1}

20:

for

i\in\Omega^{t}

do

21:

h_{i}^{t+1}:=h_{i}^{t}+\Delta_{i}^{t}-d^{t}

22:

end

for

23:

implicitly keep
h_{i}^{t+1}:=h_{i}^{t}
for all
i\in[n]\setminus\tilde{\Omega}^{t}

24:

end

for

Algorithm
2
is the form used in the analysis, while Algorithm
3
is its implementation-efficient form.
They are linked by the reparameterization

\displaystyle g_{i}^{t}:=h_{i}^{t}+q^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad i\in[n]\mathchar 59\relax\qquad g^{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{t}=h^{t}+q^{t}\mathchar 59\relax

(28)

where
h^{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{t}
.
The equivalence lemma below justifies analyzing Algorithm
2
while implementing Algorithm
3
.
It shows that the change of variables in (
28
) is exact, not an approximation.

Coupling of the randomness.

To compare the two procedures pathwise, we run them with the same random draws at every iteration:
the same index
i^{t}\in[n]
, the same subset
\Omega^{t}\subset[n]\setminus\{i^{t}\}
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1
, and the same component indices
\{j_{i}^{t}\}_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}
, where
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}:=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}
.
Once these random variables are fixed, both algorithms are deterministic.

Lemma 9

(Exact equivalence via the change of variables (
28
))
.

Assume the initial conditions are consistent with (
28
), i.e.,
q^{0}=0
and
g_{i}^{0}=h_{i}^{0}
for all
i\in[n]
.
Run Algorithm
2
and Algorithm
3
with the same random draws at every iteration.
Define the implicit variables produced by Algorithm
3
as

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{i}^{t}:=h_{i}^{t}+q^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad\widehat{g}^{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{g}_{i}^{t}=h^{t}+q^{t}.

(29)

Then, for all
t\geq 0
and all
i\in[n]
,
\widehat{g}_{i}^{t}=g_{i}^{t}
.
Consequently, Algorithms
2
and
3
generate the same iterate sequence
\{x^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
t
that
\widehat{g}_{i}^{t}=g_{i}^{t}
for all
i\in[n]
and that
h^{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{t}
.

Base case.

Since
q^{0}=0
and
g_{i}^{0}=h_{i}^{0}
for all
i\in[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{i}^{0}

\displaystyle=h_{i}^{0}+q^{0}=g_{i}^{0}.

Moreover, Algorithm
3
initializes
h^{0}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{0}
.
Thus
\widehat{g}^{0}=h^{0}+q^{0}=g^{0}
.

Induction step.

Assume that, at the beginning of iteration
t
, we have
\widehat{g}_{i}^{t}=g_{i}^{t}
for all
i\in[n]
and
h^{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{t}
.
Then
\widehat{g}^{t}=g^{t}
, and Algorithm
3
updates

\displaystyle x^{t+1}

\displaystyle=x^{t}-\gamma\left(h^{t}+q^{t}\right)=x^{t}-\gamma\widehat{g}^{t}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}\mathchar 59\relax

which is exactly the update of Algorithm
2
.
Hence both algorithms produce the same
x^{t+1}
.

For each
i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
, define

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{t}

\displaystyle:=\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle d^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{b}_{\mathrm{grp}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}.

Algorithm
3
sets
q^{t+1}=q^{t}+d^{t}
.
We now verify that the implicit variables
\widehat{g}_{i}^{t+1}=h_{i}^{t+1}+q^{t+1}
match the table updates of Algorithm
2
.

Case 1:
i=i^{t}
.

Algorithm
3
sets
h_{i^{t}}^{t+1}=\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-q^{t+1}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{i^{t}}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=h_{i^{t}}^{t+1}+q^{t+1}=\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})\mathchar 59\relax

which coincides with the update
g_{i^{t}}^{t+1}=\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})
in Algorithm
2
.

Case 2:
i\in\Omega^{t}
.

Algorithm
3
sets
h_{i}^{t+1}=h_{i}^{t}+\Delta_{i}^{t}-d^{t}
.
Thus

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{i}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=h_{i}^{t+1}+q^{t+1}=h_{i}^{t}+\Delta_{i}^{t}-d^{t}+q^{t}+d^{t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{g}_{i}^{t}+\Delta_{i}^{t}=g_{i}^{t}+\Delta_{i}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax

where the last equality uses the induction hypothesis.
This coincides with the update of Algorithm
2
.

Case 3:
i\in[n]\setminus\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
.

Algorithm
3
leaves
h_{i}
unchanged.
Hence

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{i}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=h_{i}^{t+1}+q^{t+1}=h_{i}^{t}+q^{t}+d^{t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{g}_{i}^{t}+d^{t}=g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}\mathchar 59\relax

again by the induction hypothesis.
This is exactly the update of Algorithm
2
.

Therefore
\widehat{g}_{i}^{t+1}=g_{i}^{t+1}
for all
i\in[n]
.

It remains to check that the aggregate
h^{t+1}
maintained in Algorithm
3
is equal to
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{t+1}
.
Since only entries in
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
change, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{t+1}-h^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(h_{i}^{t+1}-h_{i}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-q^{t+1}-h_{i^{t}}^{t}+\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\Delta_{i}^{t}-d^{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-h_{i^{t}}^{t}-q^{t}-d^{t}+\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}-(b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1)d^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-h_{i^{t}}^{t}-q^{t}+\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}-bd^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-h_{i^{t}}^{t}-q^{t}-\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

where the last equality uses
b_{\mathrm{grp}}d^{t}=\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}
.
This is exactly the aggregate update in Algorithm
3
.
Thus
h^{t+1}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{t+1}
, completing the induction.

Since both algorithms use the same gradient estimator,
g^{t}=\widehat{g}^{t}=h^{t}+q^{t}
, in the update of
x^{t+1}
, the iterate sequences coincide for all
t\geq 0
.
∎

Why Algorithm
3
is algorithmically more efficient.

Algorithm
2
contains two dense bookkeeping operations.
First, it adds the same vector
d^{t}
to all
n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}
table entries outside
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
.
Second, it recomputes the average
g^{t+1}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{t+1}
by passing over all
n
stored vectors.
These operations cost
\Theta(nd)
arithmetic and memory traffic per iteration.

Algorithm
3
eliminates both dense passes.
The common update
g_{i}^{t+1}=g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}
for all
i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is represented by the single shift update
q^{t+1}=q^{t}+d^{t}
, while the centered variables
h_{i}^{t}
are left unchanged for those indices.
Hence only the
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
entries in
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
are explicitly touched.

The average
h^{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{i}^{t}
is also maintained incrementally.
Since only entries in
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
change, the proof of Lemma
9
shows that

\displaystyle h^{t+1}

\displaystyle=h^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(h_{i}^{t+1}-h_{i}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=h^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-h_{i^{t}}^{t}-q^{t}-\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}\right).

This update uses only quantities already computed at iteration
t
and costs
\Theta(d)
additional arithmetic after the sampled gradients have been evaluated.

Consequently, Algorithms
2
and
3
use the same component-gradient evaluations and produce the same iterates, but Algorithm
3
avoids the
\Theta(nd)
dense bookkeeping cost of Algorithm
2
.
Its explicit table work scales with
\Theta(b_{\mathrm{grp}}d)
per iteration instead of
\Theta(nd)
.

For
b_{\mathrm{grp}}<n
, the component-gradient count per iteration is
m+1+2(b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1)
if the component
\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})
is retained while computing the full group gradient
\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})
.
Indeed, computing
\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})
costs
m
component gradients, computing
\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}
requires one additional gradient at
x^{t}
, and computing
\Delta_{i}^{t}
for each
i\in\Omega^{t}
costs two component gradients.
When
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
, this is
\Theta(m)
component-gradient evaluations per iteration.
When
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
, one may optionally omit the computation of
\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}
and the shared-shift update because
[n]\setminus\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=\emptyset
; this minor implementation shortcut does not affect the analysis.

D.2
Proofs for general nonconvex functions

The one-step analysis throughout this subsection uses the random variables generated by Algorithm
2
at iteration
t
. For ease of auditing, we collect them here; conditioned on the filtration
\mathcal{F}^{t}:=\sigma\left(x^{0}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\{g_{i}^{0}\}_{i=1}^{n}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\dots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}x^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\{g_{i}^{t}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)
, the iterate
x^{t+1}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}
is deterministic, while the remaining randomness is drawn hierarchically:
i^{t}\sim\mathcal{U}([n])
; conditional on
i^{t}
, the subset
\Omega^{t}
is uniform over the size-
(b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1)
subsets of
[n]\setminus\{i^{t}\}
; and, conditional on
(i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\Omega^{t})
, the component indices
\{j_{i}^{t}\}_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}
are independent and uniform on
[m]
.

Symbol

Definition (case
n>m
, Algorithm
2
, iteration
t
)

i^{t}

Anchor index,
i^{t}\sim\mathcal{U}([n])
; the anchored estimator is reset exactly,
g_{i^{t}}^{t+1}=\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})
.

\Omega^{t}

Recursive-update subset, sampled uniformly from
[n]\setminus\{i^{t}\}
with
|\Omega^{t}|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1
.

\tilde{\Omega}^{t}

Active set including the anchor,
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}:=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}
, so
|\tilde{\Omega}^{t}|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.

j_{i}^{t}

Component index for
i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
, sampled
j_{i}^{t}\sim\mathcal{U}([m])
(including
j_{i^{t}}^{t}
).

\Delta_{i}^{t}

Stochastic difference for
i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
,
\Delta_{i}^{t}:=\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})
.

d^{t}

Shared drift,
d^{t}:=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}
, added to every group outside
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
.

g_{i}^{t}

Group-
i
gradient estimator (table entry),
\mathcal{F}^{t}
-measurable.

g^{t}

Aggregated estimator,
g^{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{t}
, used in the step
x^{t+1}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}
.

At each iteration, every entry
g_{i}^{t+1}
follows exactly one of three mutually exclusive rules:
anchor reset
if
i=i^{t}
(so
g_{i}^{t+1}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})
),
recursive update
if
i\in\Omega^{t}
(so
g_{i}^{t+1}=g_{i}^{t}+\Delta_{i}^{t}
), and
shared drift
otherwise (so
g_{i}^{t+1}=g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}
).

Key variance lemma

As in the case
m\geq n
, the convergence proof starts with one-step estimator bounds.
The next lemma is the key technical input for Theorem
2
: it controls the table error and the aggregated estimator error for arbitrary batch size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.

Lemma 10
.

Consider Algorithm
2
(case
n>m
) with batch size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\in[n]
. Let
\mathcal{F}^{t}
be the filtration generated by the algorithm up to iteration
t
. Assume that the similarity conditions hold with parameters
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
. Then the following bounds hold for the gradient estimators:

1.
Average error.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

2.
Global estimator error.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n-2}{n}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\mathchar 59\relax

where
g^{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{t}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{t}:=\sigma\left(x^{0}\mathchar 59\relax\{g_{i}^{0}\}_{i=1}^{n}\mathchar 59\relax\dots\mathchar 59\relax x^{t}\mathchar 59\relax\{g_{i}^{t}\}_{i=1}^{n}\right)
be the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the iterates and the individual gradient estimators (table entries) up to iteration
t
; the averaged estimator
g^{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{t}
is therefore
\mathcal{F}^{t}
-measurable. Conditional on
\mathcal{F}^{t}
, the update

\displaystyle x^{t+1}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}

is deterministic. On the other hand, the construction of the table
(g_{i}^{t+1})_{i=1}^{n}
depends on the randomness generated at iteration
t
, drawn hierarchically: the index
i^{t}\sim\mathcal{U}([n])
; conditional on
i^{t}
, the subset
\Omega^{t}
uniform over the size-
(b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1)
subsets of
[n]\setminus\{i^{t}\}
; and, conditional on
(i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\Omega^{t})
, the indices
j_{i}^{t}\sim\mathcal{U}([m])
for
i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}:=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}
(including
j_{i^{t}}^{t}
), independent across
i
. Recall that
d^{t}
is defined by

\displaystyle d^{t}:=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Big(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\Big).

Fix an index
i\in[n]
. To analyze the conditional error
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]
, we apply the
law of total expectation
by conditioning on which update rule is applied to the
i
-th table entry at iteration
t
. From Algorithm
2
, there are three mutually exclusive cases:

1.

Anchor reset (
i=i^{t}
).
This event has probability
\mathbf{Prob}(i=i^{t})=\frac{1}{n}
. In this case,
g_{i}^{t+1}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})
, hence
\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}=0
.

2.

Recursive update (
i\in\Omega^{t}
).
Since
\Omega^{t}
is sampled uniformly from
[n]\setminus\{i^{t}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{Prob}(i\in\Omega^{t})=\mathbf{Prob}(i^{t}\neq i)\mathbf{Prob}(i\in\Omega^{t}\mid i^{t}\neq i)=\frac{n-1}{n}\cdot\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n-1}=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n}.

In this case,

\displaystyle g_{i}^{t+1}=g_{i}^{t}+\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

and by the same variance decomposition argument as in the case
m\geq n
(using that
j_{i}^{t}
is uniform on
[m]
so the centered increment has zero conditional mean), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\,i\in\Omega^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

3.

Drift update (
i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
).
This event has probability

\displaystyle\mathbf{Prob}(i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t})=1-\mathbf{Prob}(i=i^{t})-\mathbf{Prob}(i\in\Omega^{t})=1-\frac{1}{n}-\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n}=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}.

In this case,
g_{i}^{t+1}=g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}
, and the conditional error is
\left\|g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}
.

Combining these cases via the law of total expectation and omitting the anchor case (which contributes
\frac{1}{n}\cdot 0
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\in\Omega^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{nm}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right].

(30)

We now focus on the last conditional expectation in (
30
). If
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
, then
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=[n]
and the event
\{i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
is empty, hence this term is zero. Thus, in what follows we assume
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\leq n-1
. Using the definition of
d^{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Big(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t})\Big)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Big(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\Big)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Big(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\Big)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right].

(31)

In (
31
), we decomposed each stochastic difference into its mean part

\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})
and its centered noise part

\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})
,
and used that the latter has zero conditional mean given
\mathcal{F}^{t}
. Next, define the population mean drift (excluding
i
)

\displaystyle a_{i}^{t}:=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right).

We justify that, despite being generated in two stages, the set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is uniformly distributed.

Recall the sampling procedure in Algorithm
2
: first, we draw
i^{t}\sim\mathcal{U}([n])
. Then, conditional on
i^{t}
, we draw
\Omega^{t}
uniformly at random among all subsets of
[n]\setminus\{i^{t}\}
of cardinality
b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1
, and we set

\displaystyle\tilde{\Omega}^{t}:=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad|\tilde{\Omega}^{t}|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}.

Although this is a two-stage procedure, the resulting set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is uniform over all subsets of
[n]
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.

Indeed, fix an arbitrary subset
S\subset[n]
with
|S|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}
. The event
\{\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=S\}
occurs if and only if the anchor index satisfies
i^{t}=s
for some
s\in S
and, simultaneously, the second-stage sample satisfies
\Omega^{t}=S\setminus\{s\}
. These events are mutually exclusive over different choices of
s\in S
, hence by the law of total probability,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Prob}(\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=S)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S}\mathbf{Prob}\big(i^{t}=s\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ \Omega^{t}=S\setminus\{s\}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S}\mathbf{Prob}(i^{t}=s)\,\mathbf{Prob}\big(\Omega^{t}=S\setminus\{s\}\mid i^{t}=s\big).

Since
i^{t}
is uniform on
[n]
, we have
\mathbf{Prob}(i^{t}=s)=\frac{1}{n}
. Moreover, conditional on
i^{t}=s
, the set
\Omega^{t}
is uniform among all
\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}
subsets of
[n]\setminus\{s\}
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1
, and
S\setminus\{s\}
is one such subset. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{Prob}\big(\Omega^{t}=S\setminus\{s\}\mid i^{t}=s\big)=\frac{1}{\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}}.

Substituting these probabilities back gives

\displaystyle\mathbf{Prob}(\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=S)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in S}\frac{1}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}}=\frac{|S|}{n\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}}=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}}.

This expression does not depend on the particular choice of
S
, hence
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is uniformly distributed over all subsets of
[n]
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
, i.e., over
\binom{[n]}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}
.

We now derive the conditional uniformity used in the proof. Fix
i\in[n]
. Since
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is uniform among all size-
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
subsets of
[n]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{Prob}(i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t})=\frac{\#\{S\subset[n]\setminus\{i\}:\ |S|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}\}}{\#\{S\subset[n]:\ |S|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}\}}=\frac{\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}{\binom{n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}.

Now take any subset
S\subset[n]\setminus\{i\}
with
|S|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}
. Then
\{\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=S\}\subseteq\{i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathbf{Prob}(\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=S\mid i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbf{Prob}(\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=S)}{\mathbf{Prob}(i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}}}{\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}}=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}}=\frac{1}{\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}\mathchar 59\relax

where in the last step we used the identity
\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\binom{n-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}
.
Hence the conditional probability above is constant over all
S\subset[n]\setminus\{i\}
with
|S|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}
. Therefore, conditioned on the event
\{i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
, the set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is uniformly distributed over all subsets of
[n]\setminus\{i\}
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.

Finally, conditioning on
\mathcal{F}^{t}
does not affect this conclusion because
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is sampled at iteration
t
independently of
\mathcal{F}^{t}
. Consequently, conditioned on
\{\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\,i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
, the set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is a uniformly random subset of
[n]\setminus\{i\}
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right)\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]=a_{i}^{t}.

Therefore, applying the same variance decomposition as before (cross term vanishes because the sample mean is unbiased for
a_{i}^{t}
under the conditioning), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right)-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right].

(32)

Finally, in the noise term of (
31
), we use the independence of the indices
(j_{i^{\prime}}^{t})_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}
(conditional on
\mathcal{F}^{t}
and on the sampled set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
) to replace the squared norm of the sum by the sum of squared norms (cross terms vanish because each centered noise has zero conditional mean). Putting (
31
) and (
32
) together yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right)-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\mathchar 59\relax

(33)

which is the desired three-term expansion. In (
33
), the second term captures the sampling variance of the mean drift over the random set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
, while the third term captures the variance due to the inner sampling
(j_{i^{\prime}}^{t})
.

Let us further expand the three terms in (
33
). Throughout, we work under the conditioning

\{\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\,i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
, so that
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is a uniformly random subset of
[n]\setminus\{i\}
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
(as shown above), and all quantities depending on
(x^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}x^{t+1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}(g_{i}^{t})_{i=1}^{n})
are
\mathcal{F}^{t}
-measurable.

First term.

Recall that

\displaystyle a_{i}^{t}:=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right).

We rewrite
a_{i}^{t}
by adding and subtracting the missing
i
-th component inside the sum:

\displaystyle a_{i}^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right)-\frac{1}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right).

Using
\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x)=n\nabla f(x)
, we obtain

\displaystyle a_{i}^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)-\frac{1}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right).

Substituting this expression into the first term of (
33
) yields

\displaystyle\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.{}-\frac{1}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}.

Finally, we regroup the terms by adding and subtracting
\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})
:

\displaystyle g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\frac{1}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right)\mathchar 59\relax

and therefore

\displaystyle\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})+a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\left.{}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}.

Second term.

We now bound the sampling variance of the mean drift over the random set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
.
Assume first that
n\geq 3
(so that
n-1\geq 2
). Under
\{i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
, the set

\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is a uniformly random subset of
[n]\setminus\{i\}
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.
Apply Lemma
3
with

\displaystyle N:=n-1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad B:=b_{\mathrm{grp}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad v_{i^{\prime}}:=\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\quad(i^{\prime}\in[n]\setminus\{i\})\mathchar 59\relax\qquad v:=a_{i}^{t}.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right)-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(n-1)-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)\big((n-1)-1\big)}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right)-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n-1-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-2)}\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}.

If
n=2
, then necessarily
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=1
(since
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\leq n-1
under
\{i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
), and in this case the second term equals zero (the sample equals the population, hence the variance vanishes).

Third term.

We now compute the expectation of the averaged inner-noise term. Define, for each
i^{\prime}\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j):=\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t}).

Conditioned on
\{\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\,i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\}
, the set
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is a uniformly random subset of
[n]\setminus\{i\}
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
, and for each
i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
the index
j_{i^{\prime}}^{t}
is uniform on
[m]
and independent of the set selection. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j_{i^{\prime}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j_{i^{\prime}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}\right)\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Summing (
30
) over
i
.

Hence, taking the sum over
i=1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}n
in (
30
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\mathchar 59\relax

with

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax i\notin\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-1-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-2)}\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

(34)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

with the term (
34
) replaced by zero if
n=2
.

We now justify how the last term arises from summing the third term in (
33
) over
i
.
For
i^{\prime}\in[n]
and
j\in[m]
, recall

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j):=\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t}).

Let

\displaystyle S:=\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}.

Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\left(\frac{n}{(n-1)m}\sum_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{\prime}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}-\frac{1}{(n-1)m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(c)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\left(\frac{n}{(n-1)m}S-\frac{1}{(n-1)m}S\right)=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\cdot\frac{1}{m}S

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(d)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(e)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Here, (a) is linearity of summation, (b) uses
\sum_{i=1}^{n}1=n
, (c) is the definition of
S
,
(d) simplifies
\frac{n-1}{(n-1)m}=\frac{1}{m}
, and (e) expands
\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)
.

Moreover, assume that
n\geq 3
. We further simplify the double-sum term appearing in (
34
).
Recall that

\displaystyle a_{i}^{t}:=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right).

For each fixed
i\in[n]
, since
a_{i}^{t}
is the average of the
(n-1)
vectors

\{\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\}_{i^{\prime}\neq i}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left(\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right)=0.

Expanding the square and using the previous identity to cancel the cross term yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\left\|a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Summing this identity over
i=1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}n
gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}.

In the first term on the right-hand side, each index
i^{\prime}\in[n]
appears exactly
n-1
times in the inner sum
(since it is excluded only when
i=i^{\prime}
). Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\mathchar 59\relax

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}.

(35)

Next, define the full-average drift

\displaystyle a^{t}:=\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right).

We first use the standard identity

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}=\left\|a^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|a_{i}^{t}-a^{t}\right\|^{2}\mathchar 59\relax

which holds because
a^{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}^{t}
.
To compute the second term, we invoke Lemma
3
with
N=n
,
B=n-1
and vectors

\displaystyle v_{i}:=\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\qquad(i\in[n])\mathchar 59\relax

so that
v=a^{t}
. For each
i\in[n]
, the quantity
a_{i}^{t}
is the average of the
n-1
vectors
\{v_{i^{\prime}}\}_{i^{\prime}\neq i}
,
and the family of subsets
[n]\backslash\{i\}
is uniformly distributed over all
(n-1)
-subsets of
[n]
.
Therefore Lemma
3
yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|a_{i}^{t}-a^{t}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{n(n-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-a^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Combining the last two displays gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}=\left\|a^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{1}{n(n-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-a^{t}\right\|^{2}.

(36)

Multiplying (
36
) by
n
and substituting into (
35
), we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-n\left\|a^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{(n-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-a^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Finally, since
a^{t}
is the average of
\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})
, we have the identity

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-n\left\|a^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[n]\backslash\{i\}}\left\|\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{\prime}}(x^{t})-a_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{(n-1)^{2}}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n(n-2)}{(n-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

(37)

Substituting (
37
) into (
34
) (and recalling that the term (
34
) is replaced by zero if
n=2
),
and combining with the remaining terms derived above, we obtain that for any
n\geq 2
and any
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{(n-1)^{2}}\frac{n-1-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{bnm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

There remains to simplify the quadratic term

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}.

Expanding the squared norms and collecting terms yields

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\frac{n}{n-1}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{n^{2}}{(n-1)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2n}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\langle g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\rangle.

Substituting this expansion back and regrouping the coefficients of identical terms, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n-1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{n-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\langle g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(b_{\mathrm{grp}}+1)}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{bnm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

We now control the cross term in the previous expression. Recall that for any vectors
u\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any
\alpha>0
,
Young’s inequality gives

\displaystyle-2\langle u\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}v\rangle\leq\frac{1}{\alpha}\left\|u\right\|^{2}+\alpha\left\|v\right\|^{2}.

Choosing
\alpha=2n
and applying this with

\displaystyle u:=g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\qquad v:=\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

we obtain, for every
i\in[n]
,

\displaystyle-2\left\langle g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+2n\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Substituting this bound into the identity obtained above for

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]
, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{n-1}{n}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{2n(n-1)}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(b_{\mathrm{grp}}+1)}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}+\frac{2n(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{n-1}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{bnm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

We now simplify each term. First, since
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{n}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{2n(n-1)}\leq\frac{n-1}{n}+\frac{1}{2n}=1-\frac{1}{2n}.

Second, using
\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}+1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\leq 2
and factoring out
\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(b_{\mathrm{grp}}+1)}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}+\frac{2n(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{n-1}=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n-1}\left(\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}+1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+2n\right)\leq\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n-1}\left(2+2n\right)=\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}.

Finally, by the similarity assumptions (with parameters
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n\,\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Combining the previous inequalities yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2n(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

We now study the averaged estimator
g^{t+1}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{t+1}
.
Using the update rules of Algorithm
2
(anchor reset on
i^{t}
, recursive updates on
\Omega^{t}
,
and drift updates on
[n]\setminus\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
), we can write

\displaystyle g^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(g_{i}^{t}+\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]\backslash\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(g_{i}^{t}+d^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=g^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\right)+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}d^{t}.

Next, we incorporate the anchor index into the sum carefully. Recall that
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}
and define, for each
i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{i}^{t}

\displaystyle:=\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t}).

Since
d^{t}=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\,d^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Delta_{i}^{t}-\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\,d^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\,d^{t}-\frac{1}{n}\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\,d^{t}

\displaystyle=d^{t}-\frac{1}{n}\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}.

Therefore, the exact pathwise identity is

\displaystyle g^{t+1}

\displaystyle=g^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)+d^{t}-\frac{1}{n}\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}

\displaystyle=g^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)+d^{t}+\eta^{t}\mathchar 59\relax

where

\displaystyle\eta^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\left[\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}\right].

Conditionally on
\mathcal{F}^{t}
and
i^{t}
, the index
j_{i^{t}}^{t}
is uniform on
[m]
, and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Delta_{i^{t}}^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\eta^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=0\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\eta^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=0.

Taking conditional expectation in the corrected pathwise identity gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.d^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\eta^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

Since
i^{t}
is uniform on
[n]
and independent of
\mathcal{F}^{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.g_{i^{t}}^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=g^{t}.

Moreover, each
\Delta_{i}^{t}
is an unbiased estimator of
\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})
, and
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is uniform over size-
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
subsets of
[n]
, hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.d^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t}).

Using also
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\eta^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f(x^{t})-g^{t}\right)+\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{n-1}{n}\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right).

We now turn to the conditional second moment. Using the variance decomposition

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|Z\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=\left\|\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.Z\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]\right\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|Z-\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.Z\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]
with

Z:=g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\frac{n-1}{n}\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

We now simplify the expansion of the centered term

\displaystyle g^{t+1}

\displaystyle=g^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\right)+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\,d^{t}\mathchar 59\relax

where

\displaystyle d^{t}=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Big(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\Big)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\tilde{\Omega}^{t}:=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}.

Starting from

\displaystyle g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\frac{n-1}{n}\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\mathchar 59\relax

we substitute the above expression for
g^{t+1}
and regroup terms:

\displaystyle g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\frac{n-1}{n}\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle=\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right)-\frac{n-1}{n}g^{t}+\frac{n-1}{n}\nabla f(x^{t})+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\right)+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\,d^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\right)+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\,d^{t}+\frac{n-1}{n}\left(\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right).

We now distribute the deterministic drift term
\frac{n-1}{n}\left(\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right)
across the

\Omega^{t}
-sum and the drift part. Using
|\Omega^{t}|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1
and
|\tilde{\Omega}^{t}|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}
, we have the identity

\displaystyle\frac{n-1}{n}\left(\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right)=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right)+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\left(\nabla f(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right).

Substituting this into the previous display yields

\displaystyle g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\frac{n-1}{n}\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\left(d^{t}-\big(\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t})\big)\right).

Finally, we expand the last difference using the definition of
d^{t}
:

\displaystyle d^{t}-\big(\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Big(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})\Big)-\big(\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t})\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\Big(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\Big).

Substituting this back gives the desired decomposition:

\displaystyle g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\frac{n-1}{n}\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\frac{n-1}{n}\left(g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\left.{}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}.

Therefore, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\left.\left.\left.\!\!\!{}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

(38)

We now show explicitly how to pass from (
D.2
)
to the representation where the “outer” deviations are grouped inside one squared norm and the “inner” centered noises are separated.

For each
i\in[n]
and
j\in[m]
, define the centered inner-noise increment

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j):=\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

and define the outer deviation increment

\displaystyle\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}:=\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t}).

Then, for every
i\in[n]
and every realization of
j_{i}^{t}
, we have the identity
