\displaystyle\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})=\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}+\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t}).

(39)

We now rewrite the vector inside the conditional squared norm by using (
39
) term-by-term.
Set

\displaystyle A^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

\displaystyle B^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

\displaystyle C^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right).

Then the conditional squared norm above is
\left\|A^{t}+B^{t}+C^{t}\right\|^{2}
.
Using (
39
) in
B^{t}
and
C^{t}
yields

\displaystyle B^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

(40)

\displaystyle C^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t}).

(41)

Moreover, in
A^{t}
we add and subtract
\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})
:

\displaystyle A^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{n}\bar{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}.

(42)

Now combine (
40
), (
41
), and (
42
):

\displaystyle A^{t}+B^{t}+C^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)+\left(\frac{1}{n}\bar{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t}).

Since
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}
and
|\tilde{\Omega}^{t}|=b_{\mathrm{grp}}
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\bar{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\cdot\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax

and therefore

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\bar{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}\right)\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}.

(43)

Similarly, using again
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}=\Omega^{t}\cup\{i^{t}\}
, we can split the second noise sum as

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})=\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})+\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

(44)

and hence

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\right)\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t}).

(45)

Substituting (
43
) and (
45
) back, we obtain the exact decomposition

\displaystyle A^{t}+B^{t}+C^{t}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}}_{\text{outer part}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})}_{\text{inner noise}}.

(46)

We now expand the conditional second moment with all cross terms and show explicitly why they vanish.
Define the “outer part” and the “inner noise” as in (
D.2
):

\displaystyle R^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

\displaystyle N^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax

so that
A^{t}+B^{t}+C^{t}=R^{t}+N^{t}
.
Recall that, conditional on
\mathcal{F}^{t}
and on the sampled sets
(i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\Omega^{t})
(equivalently
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
),
the vectors
R^{t}
and
\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}
are deterministic, while the randomness in
N^{t}
comes only from the indices

\{j_{i}^{t}\}_{i\in\Omega^{t}}
and
j_{i^{t}}^{t}
.

Step 1: expand
\|R^{t}+N^{t}\|^{2}
and drop the outer–inner cross term.

We first expand the square:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|A^{t}+B^{t}+C^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|R^{t}+N^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|R^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+2\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle R^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

We show that the cross term is zero. Using the tower property with conditioning on
(\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle R^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle R^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

Conditional on
(\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t})
, the vector
R^{t}
is deterministic and thus can be pulled out:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle R^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\langle R^{t}\mathchar 59\relax\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.N^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\right\rangle.

Moreover, for each
i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
, the index
j_{i}^{t}
is uniform on
[m]
, hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]=0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.N^{t}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]+\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]=0\mathchar 59\relax

and hence

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle R^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]}_{=\,0}=0\qquad\Longrightarrow\qquad\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle R^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]}_{=\,0}=0.

Plugging this into the square expansion yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|R^{t}+N^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|R^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

Step 2: expand
\|N^{t}\|^{2}
and drop all inner-noise cross terms.

Write
N^{t}
as the sum of two parts,

\displaystyle N^{t}=N_{1}^{t}+N_{2}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad N_{1}^{t}:=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\qquad N_{2}^{t}:=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t}).

Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N_{1}^{t}+N_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+2\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle N_{1}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N_{2}^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

We show that
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle N_{1}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N_{2}^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=0
. Conditional on
(\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t})
,
the random variables
\{j_{i}^{t}\}_{i\in\Omega^{t}}
are independent of
j_{i^{t}}^{t}
, and the vectors

\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})
have zero conditional mean. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle N_{1}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N_{2}^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\langle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\mathchar 59\relax\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\langle 0\mathchar 59\relax 0\right\rangle=0\mathchar 59\relax

and therefore

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle N_{1}^{t}\mathchar 59\relax N_{2}^{t}\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]}_{=\,0}=0.

It remains to compute
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]
. Expanding the square gives

\displaystyle\left\|N_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}k\in\Omega^{t}\\
i<k\end{subarray}}\left\langle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{t}(j_{k}^{t})\right\rangle.

Conditional on
(\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t})
, the random indices
(j_{i}^{t})_{i\in\Omega^{t}}
are independent,
and each
\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})
has zero conditional mean. Hence for
i\neq k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\langle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{t}(j_{k}^{t})\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\left\langle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\mathchar 59\relax\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{t}(j_{k}^{t})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\tilde{\Omega}^{t}\right]\right\rangle=\left\langle 0\mathchar 59\relax 0\right\rangle=0.

Therefore all cross terms vanish in expectation:

\displaystyle\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}k\in\Omega^{t}\\
i<k\end{subarray}}\left\langle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\widetilde{\Delta}_{k}^{t}(j_{k}^{t})\right\rangle\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]}_{=\,0}=0\mathchar 59\relax

and we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N_{1}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

Finally,
\left\|N_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}=\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\|^{2}
, so

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N_{2}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

Combining the last displays yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|N^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

Conclusion.

Putting Step 1 and Step 2 together, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|A^{t}+B^{t}+C^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|R^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]\mathchar 59\relax

i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|A^{t}+B^{t}+C^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

Finally, substituting back
\bar{\Delta}_{i}^{t}
and
\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)
gives exactly

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\left.\left.\!\!\!{}+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

We now bound the last two noise terms.
For
i\in[n]
and
j\in[m]
, recall the shorthand

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j):=\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t}).

Noise term over
\Omega^{t}
.

Using the tower property and conditioning on
(i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\Omega^{t})
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\Omega^{t}\right]\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j_{i}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\Omega^{t}\right]\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(c)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(d)}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{Prob}(i\in\Omega^{t})\cdot\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(e)}}}{{=}}\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Here, (a)–(b) use the tower property and linearity of conditional expectation, (c) uses that conditional on

(\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\Omega^{t})
each
j_{i}^{t}
is uniform on
[m]
, (d) uses

\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}a_{i}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}\,\mathbf{Prob}(i\in\Omega^{t})
for
\mathcal{F}^{t}
-measurable
(a_{i})
,
and (e) uses
\mathbf{Prob}(i\in\Omega^{t})=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{n}
.

Noise term for the anchor index
i^{t}
.

Similarly, by conditioning first on
i^{t}
and then on
j_{i^{t}}^{t}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(a)}}}{{=}}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j_{i^{t}}^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{t}\right]\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(b)}}}{{=}}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i^{t}}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{(c)}}}{{=}}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{3}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\widetilde{\Delta}_{i}^{t}(j)\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{3}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Here, (a) is the tower property, (b) uses that conditional on
(\mathcal{F}^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}i^{t})
the index
j_{i^{t}}^{t}
is uniform on
[m]
,
and (c) uses that
i^{t}
is uniform on
[n]
and independent of
\mathcal{F}^{t}
.

Combining these two identities yields

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i\in\Omega^{t}}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i^{t}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j_{i^{t}}^{t}}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}n^{3}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{3}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Finally, applying the internal similarity condition (with parameter
\delta_{2}
) to
(x^{t+1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}x^{t})
gives

\displaystyle\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{3}m}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\left\|\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{t})+\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\leq\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Furthermore, we bound the remaining conditional second moment term by separating the two contributions inside the squared norm. Define

\displaystyle U^{t}:=\frac{1}{n}\left(\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad V^{t}:=\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right).

Then, by the elementary inequality
\left\|u+v\right\|^{2}\leq 2\left\|u\right\|^{2}+2\left\|v\right\|^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left\|U^{t}+V^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\Big|\,\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]\leq 2\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|U^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]+2\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|V^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right].

We now bound the second term
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|V^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]
using the variance formula for uniform sampling without replacement.
Apply Lemma
3
with

\displaystyle N:=n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad B:=b_{\mathrm{grp}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad v_{i}:=\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\mathchar 59\relax\qquad v:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}v_{i}=0.

Since
\tilde{\Omega}^{t}
is a uniformly random subset of
[n]
of size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
, Lemma
3
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}v_{i}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n(n-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|v_{i}\right\|^{2}.

Substituting back
v_{i}
gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\sum_{i\in\tilde{\Omega}^{t}}\left(\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right)\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n(n-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Combining the previous two displays with the bound derived above for the inner-noise terms yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n(n-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Next, since
i^{t}
is uniform on
[n]
and independent of
\mathcal{F}^{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|\nabla f_{i^{t}}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i^{t}}^{t}+g^{t}\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})-\nabla f(x^{t})-g_{i}^{t}+g^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Substituting this identity and using the similarity bound (with parameter
\delta_{1}
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n(n-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})-\nabla f(x^{t+1})-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})+\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(n-1)^{2}}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\frac{2}{n^{2}}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n-2}{n}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
2

The following theorem is the general nonconvex convergence guarantee for Algorithm
2
.
The proof uses Lemma
10
to choose a Lyapunov function whose estimator-error terms match the one-step descent recursion.

See
2

Proof of Theorem
2
.

We rely on the variance bounds established in Lemma
10
.
Specifically, item 2 of the lemma provides the bound for the global estimator error:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n-2}{n}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\mathchar 59\relax

and item 1 provides the bound for the average error:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

We also have the descent lemma for
L
-smooth functions:

\displaystyle f(x^{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

We define the Lyapunov function

\displaystyle\Psi^{t}:=f(x^{t})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}+\frac{\gamma n}{4}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{\gamma}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Let us denote the Lyapunov coefficients by
C_{1}:=\frac{\gamma n}{4}
and
C_{2}:=\frac{\gamma}{n}
.
Taking the conditional expectation of
\Psi^{t+1}
and substituting the descent lemma together with the two variance bounds above, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.f(x^{t+1})\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]-f_{\mathrm{inf}}+C_{1}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\begin{aligned} &f(x^{t})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\\
&\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\end{aligned}}_{\text{Descent lemma}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{1}\Bigg[\frac{n-2}{n}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad+C_{2}\Bigg[n\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+n\Bigg(\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\Bigg)\Bigg].

We now group the terms by the error components
\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}
,
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}
, and the stepsize
\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}+C_{1}\frac{n-2}{n}\right)\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(C_{1}\frac{2}{n^{3}}+C_{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+C_{1}\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}+C_{2}n\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+C_{1}\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}+C_{2}n\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}\Bigg)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Substituting
C_{1}=\frac{\gamma n}{4}
and
C_{2}=\frac{\gamma}{n}
yields the simplifications

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}+C_{1}\frac{n-2}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{\gamma n}{4}\cdot\frac{n-2}{n}=\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{\gamma(n-2)}{4}=\frac{\gamma n}{4}=C_{1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

\displaystyle C_{1}\frac{2}{n^{3}}+C_{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma n}{4}\cdot\frac{2}{n^{3}}+\frac{\gamma}{n}\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)=\frac{\gamma}{2n^{2}}+\frac{\gamma}{n}-\frac{\gamma}{2n^{2}}=\frac{\gamma}{n}=C_{2}\mathchar 59\relax

and hence the coefficients of the error terms match exactly those in
\Psi^{t}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{t}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\gamma\left(\frac{n(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\right)\delta_{1}^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\gamma\left(\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{4b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\right)\delta_{2}^{2}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Using
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\geq 1
, we have
\frac{n}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\leq\frac{n}{2}
, and thus

\displaystyle\frac{n(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n-1}\left(\frac{n}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+2(n+1)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{2(n-1)}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{4b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}=\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{4b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\Psi^{t}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\,\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{\gamma}{2}\,\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

Hence, the last term is nonpositive whenever

\displaystyle L-\frac{1}{\gamma}+\gamma\left(\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 0\mathchar 59\relax

that is, whenever

\displaystyle\left(\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\right)\gamma^{2}+L\gamma\leq 1.

By Lemma
6
, applied with

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle:=\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad b:=L\mathchar 59\relax

it therefore suffices to impose

\displaystyle\gamma\leq\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}}\right)^{-1}\mathchar 59\relax

which is exactly the stepsize condition in the theorem.

Under this stepsize condition, we obtain the recursion

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[\left.\Psi^{t+1}\,\right|\mathcal{F}^{t}\right]\leq\Psi^{t}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}.

Unrolling this inequality for
t=0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}T
and using
\Psi^{t}\geq 0
yields

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{t=0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\Psi^{0}.

Finally, by defining
\tilde{x}^{T}
as
x^{t}
for
t
chosen uniformly at random in
\{0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}T\}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(\tilde{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]=\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{2\Psi^{0}}{\gamma(T+1)}\mathchar 59\relax

which concludes the proof.
∎

The next corollary converts Theorem
2
into iteration and component-gradient complexity with arbitrary initialization.
It then specializes the bound to the natural choice
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
, which is admissible in the regime
n>m
.

See
2

Proof of Corollary
2
.

By Theorem
2
, for the chosen value of
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
and stepsize

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}}\right)^{-1}\mathchar 59\relax

we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(\tilde{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\frac{2\Psi_{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}^{0}}{\gamma(T+1)}.

Thus, if
T+1\geq 2\Psi_{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}^{0}/(\gamma\epsilon)
, then
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(\tilde{x}^{T})\right\|^{2}\right]\leq\epsilon
. Equivalently, the required number of iterations satisfies

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\frac{\Psi_{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}^{0}}{\gamma\epsilon}\right)

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}}\right)\frac{\Psi_{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}^{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

It remains to count component-gradient evaluations. At each iteration of Algorithm
2
, the algorithm computes one full group gradient
\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})
, which costs
m
component-gradient evaluations. In addition, the update uses one extra component-gradient evaluation for the selected group and two component-gradient evaluations for each of the other
b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1
groups in the batch. Hence, for
b_{\mathrm{grp}}<n
, the per-iteration cost is

\displaystyle m+1+2(b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1)

\displaystyle=m+2b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1={\cal O}\left(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}}\right).

When
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
, the extra component gradient for the selected group is not needed, and the cost is
m+2(b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1)
, which is still
{\cal O}\left(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}}\right)
. Therefore, multiplying the iteration bound by the per-iteration cost gives

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left((m+b_{\mathrm{grp}})\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}}\right)\frac{\Psi_{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}^{0}}{\epsilon}\right)

component-gradient evaluations, excluding initialization.

Now specialize to
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
. Since
n>m
, this choice is admissible. Define

\displaystyle A_{1}(m)

\displaystyle:=\frac{(n-m)(5n+4)}{n-1}\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle A_{2}(m)

\displaystyle:=\frac{n^{2}+2n-7m+4m^{2}}{2mn}.

Then the post-initialization component-gradient complexity is

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left(m\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}A_{1}(m)+\delta_{2}^{2}A_{2}(m)}\right)\frac{\Psi_{m}^{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

Using
\sqrt{a+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}_{\mathrm{grp}}
, we get

\displaystyle m\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}A_{1}(m)+\delta_{2}^{2}A_{2}(m)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq mL+m\delta_{1}\sqrt{A_{1}(m)}+m\delta_{2}\sqrt{A_{2}(m)}.

Since
n>m
, we have
n\geq 2
and

\displaystyle\frac{5n+4}{n-1}

\displaystyle=5+\frac{9}{n-1}\leq 14.

Therefore

\displaystyle A_{1}(m)

\displaystyle\leq 14(n-m)\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle m\delta_{1}\sqrt{A_{1}(m)}

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(m\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}\right).

Similarly, using
m\leq n
and
n\geq 2
,

\displaystyle n^{2}+2n-7m+4m^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}+2n+4m^{2}\leq 7n^{2}\mathchar 59\relax

and hence

\displaystyle A_{2}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{7n}{2m}\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle m\delta_{2}\sqrt{A_{2}(m)}

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right).

Combining the last three displays gives

\displaystyle m\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}A_{1}(m)+\delta_{2}^{2}A_{2}(m)}\right)

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(mL+m\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right).

Thus, with
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
, the expected component-gradient complexity is

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left(\left(mL+m\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Psi_{m}^{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

Now, we assume exact initialization.

For the choice
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
, the stepsize condition in Theorem
2
gives

\displaystyle\gamma

\displaystyle=\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-m)(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7m+4m^{2}}{2mn}}\right)^{-1}.

The exact initialization
g_{i}^{0}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})
for every
i\in[n]
implies

\displaystyle g^{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{0}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})=\nabla f(x^{0}).

Therefore, both initialization-error terms in the Lyapunov quantity from Theorem
2
vanish:

\displaystyle\left\|g^{0}-\nabla f(x^{0})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=0\mathchar 59\relax

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{0}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=0.

Hence, for
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
,

\displaystyle\Psi_{m}^{0}

\displaystyle=f(x^{0})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}=\Delta_{0}.

Applying Corollary
2
with
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
and
\Psi_{m}^{0}=\Delta_{0}
gives the post-initialization iteration complexity

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}\frac{(n-m)(5n+4)}{n-1}+\delta_{2}^{2}\frac{n^{2}+2n-7m+4m^{2}}{2mn}}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

Using
\sqrt{a+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\leq\sqrt{a}+\sqrt{b}_{\mathrm{grp}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\delta_{1}\sqrt{\frac{(n-m)(5n+4)}{n-1}}+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}+2n-7m+4m^{2}}{2mn}}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

Since
n>m
, we have
n\geq 2
and

\displaystyle\frac{5n+4}{n-1}

\displaystyle=5+\frac{9}{n-1}\leq 14\mathchar 59\relax

and hence

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{(n-m)(5n+4)}{n-1}}

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\sqrt{n-m}\right).

Moreover, using
m\leq n
and
n\geq 2
,

\displaystyle n^{2}+2n-7m+4m^{2}

\displaystyle\leq n^{2}+2n+4m^{2}\leq 7n^{2}\mathchar 59\relax

so that

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n^{2}+2n-7m+4m^{2}}{2mn}}

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}\right).

Combining the last displays yields

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}+\sqrt{\frac{n}{m}}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

The same Corollary
2
gives the expected post-initialization component-gradient complexity

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left(\left(mL+m\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

It remains to add the initialization cost. Computing
g_{i}^{0}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})
requires evaluating the full group gradient

\displaystyle\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x^{0})\mathchar 59\relax

which costs
m
component-gradient evaluations for a fixed group
i
. Repeating this for all
i\in[n]
costs

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}m

\displaystyle=mn

component-gradient evaluations. Therefore, the total expected component-gradient complexity, including initialization, is

\displaystyle{\cal O}\left(nm+\left(mL+m\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

∎

D.3
Analysis under the Polyak–Łojasiewicz condition

The PŁ argument for the regime
n>m
is parallel.
The only change is in the constant controlling the Lyapunov recursion: both similarity parameters now enter through the batch-dependent quantity
\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}
below.
Under Assumption
5
, this gives an
O\left(\cdot\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)
dependence for the objective gap, rather than the
O\left(\cdot\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)
dependence of the general nonconvex stationarity result.
As above, the theorem is stated globally, while the proof only invokes PŁ along the iterates; local PŁ regions are therefore sufficient whenever the trajectory stays inside them.

The next theorem is the PŁ analogue of Theorem
2
.
The quantity
\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}
summarizes the batch-size-dependent similarity terms that enter the PŁ Lyapunov recursion.

Theorem 4

(Linear convergence under PŁ, case
n>m
)
.

Let Assumptions
1
,
2
,
3
,
4
, and
5
hold,
where Assumption
5
holds with parameter
\mu>0
.
Run Algorithm
2
with batch size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\in[n]
, arbitrary initial estimators

g_{1}^{0}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}g_{n}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and
g^{0}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}^{0}
.
Define

\displaystyle\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}:=\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{n-1}\left(\frac{n}{3b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+4(n+1)\right)\delta_{1}^{2}+\frac{n^{2}+10n-23b_{\mathrm{grp}}+12b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{3b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}.

(47)

Let the stepsize be set as

0<\gamma\leq\min\left\{\left(L+\sqrt{2\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}\right)^{-1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\,\frac{1}{3\mu n}\right\}
.
Then, for every
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]\leq(1-\gamma\mu)^{T}\left(\Psi_{\mathrm{PL}}^{0}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

(48)

where

\Psi_{\mathrm{PL}}^{0}:=f(x^{0})-f^{*}+\frac{\gamma n}{3}\left\|g^{0}-\nabla f(x^{0})\right\|^{2}+4\gamma\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{0}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})\right\|^{2}.

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Let us fix constants
\nu\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\rho\in[0\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\infty)
that we will define later.
We start from the descent lemma for
L
-smooth functions, applied with
x^{t+1}=x^{t}-\gamma g^{t}
:

\displaystyle f(x^{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq f(x^{t})+\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}-\frac{\gamma}{2}\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right)\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}.

(49)

Taking expectation and adding
\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]
and

\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]
to both sides, and then using Lemma
10
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+\left(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\begin{aligned} &\frac{n-2}{n}\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}+\frac{2}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\begin{aligned} &\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}+\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\end{aligned}\right].

Rearranging the terms yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\nu\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\rho\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\frac{\gamma}{2}+\nu\frac{n-2}{n}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\rho\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)+\nu\frac{2}{n^{2}}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\Bigg(\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\nu\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}+\nu\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}+\rho\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}+\rho\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right].

(50)

We now choose

\displaystyle\nu:=\frac{\gamma n}{3}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\rho:=4\gamma.

(51)

With this choice, the coefficient of
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|x^{t+1}-x^{t}\right\|^{2}\right]
in (
50
) becomes

\displaystyle\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\nu\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}(n-1)}\delta_{1}^{2}+\nu\frac{n^{2}-2n+b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n^{2}}\delta_{2}^{2}+\rho\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(n+1)}{n-1}\delta_{1}^{2}+\rho\frac{n-2b_{\mathrm{grp}}+b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\gamma\Bigg(\frac{2(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})}{n-1}\left(\frac{n}{3b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+4(n+1)\right)\delta_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{n^{2}+10n-23b_{\mathrm{grp}}+12b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{3b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\delta_{2}^{2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{L}{2}-\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\gamma\,\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\mathchar 59\relax

where
\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}
is defined in (
47
).

Thus, the term is nonpositive whenever

\displaystyle L-\frac{1}{\gamma}+2\gamma\,\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\leq 0\mathchar 59\relax

that is, whenever

\displaystyle 2\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\gamma^{2}+L\gamma\leq 1.

By Lemma
6
, applied with

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle:=2\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad b:=L\mathchar 59\relax

it therefore suffices to impose

\displaystyle\gamma\leq\left(L+\sqrt{2\Xi_{n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}\right)^{-1}\mathchar 59\relax

which is exactly the first constraint in the stepsize condition of Theorem
4
.

Next, with the choice (
51
), we claim that if
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{3\mu n}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}+\nu\frac{n-2}{n}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\mu)\nu\mathchar 59\relax

(52)

\displaystyle\rho\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)+\nu\frac{2}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma\mu)\rho.

(53)

Indeed, for (
52
),

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma}{2}+\nu\frac{n-2}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{2}+\nu-\frac{2\nu}{n}=\frac{\gamma}{2}+\frac{\gamma n}{3}-\frac{2\gamma}{3}=\frac{\gamma n}{3}-\frac{\gamma}{6}=\nu-\frac{\gamma}{6}

\displaystyle\leq\nu-\gamma^{2}\mu\,\frac{n}{3}=(1-\gamma\mu)\nu\mathchar 59\relax

where the inequality holds whenever
\gamma\mu\leq\frac{1}{2n}
, which is implied by
\gamma\leq\frac{1}{3\mu n}
.
For (
53
), we have

\displaystyle\rho\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)+\nu\frac{2}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=4\gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{2n}\right)+\frac{\gamma n}{3}\cdot\frac{2}{n^{2}}

\displaystyle=4\gamma-\frac{2\gamma}{n}+\frac{2\gamma}{3n}=4\gamma-\frac{4\gamma}{3n}=\rho\left(1-\frac{1}{3n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\rho(1-\gamma\mu)=(1-\gamma\mu)\rho\mathchar 59\relax

where the inequality is equivalent to
\gamma\mu\leq\frac{1}{3n}
.

Substituting (
51
), (
52
), and (
53
)
into (
50
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\frac{\gamma n}{3}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t+1}-\nabla f(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]+4\gamma\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t+1}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t+1})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(1-\gamma\mu)\left(\frac{\gamma n}{3}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+4\gamma\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]\right).

(54)

Finally, define the nonnegative sequence

\displaystyle\Psi^{t}:=\frac{n}{3}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|g^{t}-\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+4\,{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|g_{i}^{t}-\nabla f_{i}(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right].

(55)

Then (
54
) reads

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t+1})\right]+\gamma\Psi^{t+1}\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{t})\right]-\frac{\gamma}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\|\nabla f(x^{t})\right\|^{2}\right]+(1-\gamma\mu)\gamma\Psi^{t}.

In view of Lemma
8
(with
C=0
), we conclude that for every
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[f(x^{T})-f^{*}\right]\leq(1-\gamma\mu)^{T}\left((f(x^{0})-f^{*})+\gamma\Psi^{0}\right).

Substituting (
55
) at
t=0
yields (
48
).
∎

D.4
Optimizing the batch size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}

The preceding convergence bound in the regime
n>m
depends on the minibatch size
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.
This raises a practical question: which value of
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
should be used to minimize the total number of component-gradient evaluations?
In this section, we optimize the bound from Theorem
2
with respect to
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.

We use the upper bound on the total number of component-gradient evaluations as the criterion for choosing
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
.
Assume that
g_{i}^{0}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})
for all
i\in[n]
, so that

\displaystyle\Psi^{0}=\Delta_{0}:=f(x^{0})-f_{\mathrm{inf}}.

Let
\mathrm{IFO}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})
denote the total number of component-gradient evaluations
\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}
needed to obtain an
\epsilon
-stationary point.

By Theorem
2
, with

\displaystyle A_{1}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})

\displaystyle:=\frac{(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})(5n+4)}{n-1}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad A_{2}(b_{\mathrm{grp}}):=\frac{n^{2}+2n-7b_{\mathrm{grp}}+4b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}\mathchar 59\relax

it is sufficient to take

\displaystyle T+1={\cal O}\left(\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}A_{1}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})+\delta_{2}^{2}A_{2}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

Furthermore, each iteration computes
m+2b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1
component gradients (and
m+2b_{\mathrm{grp}}-2
when
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
, which changes the final complexity only by an additive constant). Since the initialization
g_{i}^{0}=\nabla f_{i}(x^{0})
costs
nm
component gradients, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{IFO}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})={\cal O}\left(nm+\left(m+2b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1\right)\left(L+\sqrt{\delta_{1}^{2}A_{1}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})+\delta_{2}^{2}A_{2}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

(56)

We now simplify the dependence on
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
. Since
n\geq 2
,

\displaystyle 5(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})\leq A_{1}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})\leq 14(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})\mathchar 59\relax

because
\frac{5n+4}{n-1}\in[5\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}14]
for all
n\geq 2
. Also,

\displaystyle A_{2}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})=\frac{n}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}-\frac{7}{2n}+\frac{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}.

For
1\leq b_{\mathrm{grp}}\leq n
, the last three terms are jointly nonnegative. Over a common denominator,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}-\frac{7}{2n}+\frac{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}=\frac{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}-\frac{7}{2}b_{\mathrm{grp}}+n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}n}.

As a function of
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
, the numerator
2b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{2}-\frac{7}{2}b_{\mathrm{grp}}+n
is a convex parabola with vertex at
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=\frac{7}{8}<1
, hence nondecreasing on
[1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}n]
; its minimum there is attained at
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=1
and equals
n-\frac{3}{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}>0
, using
n\geq 2
. Therefore
\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}-\frac{7}{2n}+\frac{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{n}>0
.
Hence

\displaystyle A_{2}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})\geq\frac{n}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}.

On the other hand, because
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\leq n
,

\displaystyle A_{2}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+\frac{1}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+2\leq\frac{n}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+\frac{n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+\frac{2n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}=\frac{7n}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle A_{1}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})=\Theta(n-b_{\mathrm{grp}})\mathchar 59\relax\qquad A_{2}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})=\Theta\!\left(\frac{n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\right).

Since also
m+2b_{\mathrm{grp}}-1=\Theta(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}})
and
\sqrt{u+v}=\Theta(\sqrt{u}+\sqrt{v})
for all
u\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}v\geq 0
, (
56
) becomes

\displaystyle\mathrm{IFO}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})=\Theta\left(nm+\Phi(b_{\mathrm{grp}})\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\Phi(b_{\mathrm{grp}}):=(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}})\left(L+\delta_{1}\sqrt{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}+\delta_{2}\sqrt{\frac{n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}\right).

(57)

Thus, up to absolute constants, minimizing the total oracle complexity is equivalent to minimizing the one-dimensional function
\Phi(b_{\mathrm{grp}})
over
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\in[n]
, i.e.,

\displaystyle b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{\star}\in\argmin_{b_{\mathrm{grp}}\in[n]}\Phi(b_{\mathrm{grp}}).

(58)

The continuous relaxation already shows that the optimal batch size is parameter dependent. Indeed, for
b_{\mathrm{grp}}\in[1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}n]
,

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})=L+\delta_{1}\frac{2n-m-3b_{\mathrm{grp}}}{2\sqrt{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}+\delta_{2}\frac{\sqrt{n}\,(b_{\mathrm{grp}}-m)}{2b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{3/2}}.

Hence there is no universal closed-form choice of
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
that minimizes all terms simultaneously. This is also clear from the three contributions in (
57
):

\displaystyle\Phi_{L}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})

\displaystyle:=(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}})L\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

\displaystyle\Phi_{\delta_{1}}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})

\displaystyle:=(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}})\sqrt{n-b_{\mathrm{grp}}}\,\delta_{1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}

\displaystyle\Phi_{\delta_{2}}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})

\displaystyle:=(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}})\sqrt{\frac{n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}\,\delta_{2}.

The smoothness term
\Phi_{L}(b_{\mathrm{grp}})
is minimized by the smallest possible batch size. The
\delta_{2}
-term is minimized exactly at
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
, because

\displaystyle(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}})\sqrt{\frac{n}{b_{\mathrm{grp}}}}=\sqrt{n}\left(\sqrt{b}_{\mathrm{grp}}+\frac{m}{\sqrt{b}_{\mathrm{grp}}}\right)\geq 2\sqrt{nm}\mathchar 59\relax

(59)

with equality if and only if
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
. Finally, the
\delta_{1}
-term is completely eliminated by taking
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
.

Two particularly relevant choices are therefore
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
and
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
. If
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
, then (
57
) yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{IFO}(m)={\cal O}\left(nm+\left(mL+m\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}+\sqrt{nm}\,\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

(60)

where we used (
59
). If
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
, then
A_{1}(n)=0
and

\displaystyle A_{2}(n)=\frac{n^{2}+2n-7n+4n^{2}}{2n^{2}}=\frac{5}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{n}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

so (
56
) gives

\displaystyle\mathrm{IFO}(n)={\cal O}\left(nm+n\left(L+\delta_{2}\right)\frac{\Delta_{0}}{\epsilon}\right).

(61)

Therefore, choosing
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
is
not
optimal in general. At the level of the upper bounds (
60
)–(
61
), the choice
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
can only improve upon
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
in the regime

\displaystyle m\sqrt{n-m}\,\delta_{1}=\Omega\left((n-m)L+(n-\sqrt{nm})\delta_{2}\right)\mathchar 59\relax

that is, when the benefit of removing the
\delta_{1}
-term completely outweighs the larger
n
-scale smoothness cost and the larger
\delta_{2}
-contribution. Otherwise, the balanced choice
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
is preferable. In summary, the optimal batch size in the case
n>m
is the minimizer
b_{\mathrm{grp}}^{\star}
in (
58
), and the explicit choices
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
and
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
capture the two main regimes of interest. Rather than reporting a separate ablation, the batch-size grid search of Appendix
E.6
navigates this tradeoff—per-iteration cost
{\cal O}\left(m+b_{\mathrm{grp}}\right)
growing with
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
, the
\delta_{2}
-term minimized at
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=m
, and the
\delta_{1}
-term removed only at
b_{\mathrm{grp}}=n
—empirically, selecting the
b_{\mathrm{grp}}
that minimizes oracle complexity on each instance.

Appendix E
Supplementary Experimental Details

This appendix extends the experimental evaluation presented in Section
4
, providing comprehensive details on the dataset generation process, the formal Hessian interpretation of the nested similarity parameters, and our estimation protocols.

Our empirical evaluation is organized around the nested finite-sum structure

\displaystyle f(x):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{i}(x)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad f_{i}(x):=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)\mathchar 59\relax

(62)

in which the partition into
n
groups of size
m
is
chosen by the experimenter
. Our theory predicts that optimization is highly sensitive to this choice: it is beneficial to group the data so that the within-group similarity is high (small
\delta_{2}
), while the influence of across-group similarity (
\delta_{1}
) depends on the dimensional regime (
m\geq n
versus
n>m
). The rest of this appendix specifies the logistic-regression task and the synthetic data used in our experiments, derives the Hessian-based characterization of
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
, describes both theoretical and empirical procedures for estimating
L
,
\delta_{1}
, and
\delta_{2}
, and reports hardware and hyperparameter-tuning details.

E.1
Binary logistic regression: loss, gradient, Hessian, and data geometry

Our empirical study uses binary logistic regression augmented with a smooth nonconvex regularizer. Each component takes the form

\displaystyle f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)\;:=\;\ell(x;a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j})+\lambda\,r(x)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\ell(x;a\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y):=\log\bigl(1+\exp(-y\,a^{\top}x)\bigr)\mathchar 59\relax

(63)

where
y_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\in\{\pm 1\}
are the labels and
a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are the feature vectors. The regularizer
r:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
introduces nonconvexity while remaining twice continuously differentiable:

\displaystyle r(x):=\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\frac{x_{\ell}^{2}}{1+x_{\ell}^{2}}.

(64)

Letting
\sigma(t):=(1+e^{-t})^{-1}
denote the standard sigmoid, the gradient and Hessian of the unregularized logistic loss are

\displaystyle\nabla\ell(x;a\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y)

\displaystyle=-y\,a\,\sigma(-y\,a^{\top}x)\mathchar 59\relax

(65)

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\ell(x;a\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y)

\displaystyle=\alpha(x;a\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y)\,aa^{\top}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\alpha(x;a\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y):=\sigma(z)\bigl(1-\sigma(z)\bigr)\mathchar 59\relax\quad z:=-y\,a^{\top}x\mathchar 59\relax

(66)

so that
0<\alpha(x;a\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y)\leq\tfrac{1}{4}
for all
x
,
a
, and
y
. For the regularizer (
64
), the coordinate-wise gradient and Hessian are

\displaystyle\left(\nabla r(x)\right)_{\ell}=\frac{2x_{\ell}}{(1+x_{\ell}^{2})^{2}}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\nabla^{2}r(x)=\Diag\!\left(\frac{2(1-3x_{\ell}^{2})}{(1+x_{\ell}^{2})^{3}}\right)_{\ell=1}^{d}.

(67)

Combining these contributions, the full component gradient and Hessian are

\displaystyle\nabla f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)

\displaystyle=-y_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\,a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\,\sigma(-y_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\,a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}^{\top}x)+\lambda\nabla r(x)\mathchar 59\relax

(68)

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)\,a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}^{\top}+\lambda\nabla^{2}r(x)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad 0<\alpha_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)\leq\tfrac{1}{4}.

(69)

Two structural properties of (
69
) drive the rest of the analysis. First, the data-dependent term is a scaled rank-one matrix whose magnitude is governed by the feature norm,
\left\|aa^{\top}\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}=\left\|a\right\|^{2}
. Second, although the regularizer is nonconvex and
\nabla^{2}r(x)
depends on
x
, it is
identical across all components
in (
63
). Consequently, the regularizer contribution cancels exactly in every nested Hessian difference, such as
\nabla^{2}f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)-\nabla^{2}f_{i}(x)
and
\nabla^{2}f_{i}(x)-\nabla^{2}f(x)
. Therefore, the similarity parameters
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
are governed entirely by the underlying
data geometry
(through the rank-one terms) and by the chosen grouping partition.

As a concrete illustration, within a fixed group
i
the deviation of an individual sample’s Hessian from the local group average reads

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)-\nabla^{2}f_{i}(x)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)\,a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}^{\top}-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{k=1}^{m}\alpha_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}k}(x)\,a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}k}a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}k}^{\top}.

(70)

This isolates the precise structural quantity that drives the Hessian-based characterization of similarity established in Lemma
11
.

E.2
Partition regimes and synthetic datasets

To empirically validate our theory, we generate synthetic grouped datasets that natively respect the nested finite-sum structure of (
62
). We investigate two aspect ratios,
(n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}m)=(50\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}250)
and
(n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}m)=(250\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}50)
, corresponding to the
m\geq n
and
n>m
regimes, respectively.

For each aspect ratio, we construct four datasets that realize all four quadrants of the qualitative similarity space:

\displaystyle(\delta_{1}\text{ small}\mathchar 59\relax\delta_{2}\text{ small})\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad(\delta_{1}\text{ small}\mathchar 59\relax\delta_{2}\text{ large})\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad(\delta_{1}\text{ large}\mathchar 59\relax\delta_{2}\text{ small})\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\qquad(\delta_{1}\text{ large}\mathchar 59\relax\delta_{2}\text{ large}).

The datasets are generated directly in their grouped form, so a sample
(i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j)
belongs to group
i
by construction and no post-hoc clustering step is required. To evaluate methods that operate on a single-level finite sum, the same data are also stored in flattened format.

The base task is the binary logistic regression with the nonconvex regularizer
r(x):=\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}x_{\ell}^{2}/(1+x_{\ell}^{2})
introduced in (
63
). For each feature–label pair
(a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}y_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{-1\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}+1\}
, the component loss is

\displaystyle f_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}(x)

\displaystyle=\log\bigl(1+\exp(-y_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}^{\top}x)\bigr)+\lambda\,r(x)\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\lambda=200.

Because the same regularizer
r(x)
is added to every component, it cancels exactly in the Hessian differences used to define
\delta_{1}
and
\delta_{2}
, while continuing to contribute to the global and maximum smoothness constants
L
and
L_{\max}
.

The feature vectors
a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}
are generated using a hierarchical latent-mixture model. We define
K
latent components partitioned evenly into
T
macro-families, such that each family contains
q=K/T
components. We let

\displaystyle t(k)

\displaystyle:=1+\left\lfloor\frac{k-1}{q}\right\rfloor\mathchar 59\relax\qquad k\in[K]\mathchar 59\relax

denote the macro-family index of component
k
. We sample
T+K
orthonormal directions

\displaystyle v_{1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}v_{T}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}u_{1}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\ldots\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}u_{K}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

and establish the cluster centers as:

\displaystyle c_{r}

\displaystyle:=r_{\mathrm{inter}}v_{r}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\mu_{k}:=c_{t(k)}+r_{\mathrm{intra}}u_{k}.

The scalar
r_{\mathrm{inter}}
dictates the separation between distinct macro-families, while
r_{\mathrm{intra}}
controls the variance among latent components within the same macro-family.

Each group
i\in[n]
is assigned a prototype mixture vector
\beta_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{K}
. To prevent degenerate zero probabilities when sampling, we smooth this prototype using a small constant
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{dir}}
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\beta}_{i}

\displaystyle=(1-\varepsilon_{\mathrm{dir}})\beta_{i}+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{dir}}\frac{\mathbf{1}}{K}.

The data points are then sampled hierarchically:

\displaystyle\pi_{i}

\displaystyle\sim\mathrm{Dirichlet}(\alpha_{\mathrm{dir}}\widetilde{\beta}_{i})\mathchar 59\relax\qquad z_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\sim\mathrm{Categorical}(\pi_{i})\mathchar 59\relax\qquad a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mu_{z_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}}\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}\sigma_{\mathrm{obs}}^{2}I_{d}).

All datasets are generated in dimension
d=1000
. The
(n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}m)=(50\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}250)
configuration uses seed
42
, and the
(n\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}m)=(250\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}50)
configuration uses seed
142
.

Labels are generated via a Gaussian teacher logistic model. The teacher direction
x_{\star}
is normalized and rescaled by the inverse median absolute raw logit to ensure class probabilities do not degenerate. Labels are sampled according to:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(y_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}=1\mid a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+\exp(-a_{i\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}j}^{\top}x_{\star})}.

If the empirical positive-class fraction falls outside the
[0.3\mathchar 59\relax 0.7]
range, the teacher direction is resampled.

To formalize the four structural regimes, let
e_{k}
denote the
k
-th standard basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
, and define the base indices:

\displaystyle a_{r}

\displaystyle:=(r-1)q+1\mathchar 59\relax\qquad s(i):=1+((i-1)\bmod T).

By manipulating only the prototype mixture assignments
\beta_{i}
and the geometric scalars, we construct the four distinct data profiles:

Small
\delta_{1}
, Small
\delta_{2}
.

All groups share the same tightly concentrated prototype:

\displaystyle\beta_{i}

\displaystyle=0.95e_{1}+0.05e_{2}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\forall i\in[n].

Consequently, the groups are mutually similar (
\delta_{1}
is small), and each group is internally coherent (
\delta_{2}
is small).

Small
\delta_{1}
, Large
\delta_{2}
.

All groups share the same broad prototype, activating one component per macro-family:

\displaystyle\beta_{i}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{r=1}^{T}e_{a_{r}}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\forall i\in[n].

Because the mixture is identical across groups,
\delta_{1}
remains small; however, because each group mixes broadly separated macro-families,
\delta_{2}
is fundamentally large.

Large
\delta_{1}
, Small
\delta_{2}
.

Groups are assigned strictly different macro-family identities, yet each group remains concentrated exclusively inside its assigned family:

\displaystyle\beta_{i}

\displaystyle=0.95e_{a_{s(i)}}+0.05e_{a_{s(i)}+1}.

Thus, different groups represent distinct statistical populations (large
\delta_{1}
), while remaining internally homogeneous (small
\delta_{2}
).

Large
\delta_{1}
, Large
\delta_{2}
.

For this highly heterogeneous regime, we scale the dimensions to
K=64
,
T=8
, and
q=8
. We define:

\displaystyle\gamma_{r}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2}e_{a_{r}}+\frac{1}{2}e_{a_{r}+4}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad r\in[T]\mathchar 59\relax

and construct cyclic family-pair prototypes:

\displaystyle\beta^{(r)}

\displaystyle:=0.7\gamma_{r}+0.3\gamma_{r+1}\mathchar 59\relax\qquad\gamma_{T+1}:=\gamma_{1}.

Each group is assigned
\beta_{i}=\beta^{(s(i))}
. This ensures each group maintains a distinct macro-family anchor (driving
\delta_{1}
high), while simultaneously mixing widely separated components (driving
\delta_{2}
high).

Setting

(\delta_{1}\text{ small}\mathchar 59\relax\delta_{2}\text{ large})

32

4

1500

10^{-3}

0.02

(30\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}0.5)

n>m

(\delta_{1}\text{ large}\mathchar 59\relax\delta_{2}\text{ small})

32

4

1500

10^{-4}

0.005

(30\mathord{\mathchar 59\relax}0.01)

n>m

(\delta_{1}\text{ large}\mathchar 59\relax\delta_{2}\text{ large})

64

8
